      "literal": "3.1.2. Fon tions sous-harmoniques.\nDéfinition 3.1.5. \u0016 Soit Ω un ouvert de X ; une fon tion u : Ω → R ∪ {−∞} est dite\nsous-harmonique si elle est semi- ontinue supérieurement, n'est identiquement égale à −∞\nsur au une omposante onnexe de Ω et satisfait à la ondition suivante : pour tout domaine\nstri tement k-a\u001enoïde Y dans X et toute fon tion h ∈ H(Y ),\n                                  (u|∂Y ≤ h|∂Y ) =⇒ (u|Y ≤ h).\n\n     L'assertion suivante n'est pas surprenante.\nProposition 3.1.6. \u0016 Soient Ω ⊂ X un ouvert et f ∈ Γ(Ω, OX ) une se tion inversible\nsur un ouvert dense de Ω ; la fon tion log |f | est sous-harmonique sur Ω.\nDémonstration. La fon tion log |f | est ontinue sur Ω et, f étant inversible sur un ouvert\ndense, n'est identiquement égale à −∞ sur au une des omposante onnexes de Ω.\n   Soient Y ⊂ Ω un domaine stri tement k-a\u001enoïde et h ∈ H(Y ) une fon tion majorant log |f |\nsur Γ(Y ) = ∂Y ; puisqu'il su\u001et de prouver l'inégalité log |f ||Y ≤ h après avoir e\u001be tué une\nextension \u001cnie k′ de k, nous pouvons supposer que Y est la \u001cbre générique d'une S - ourbe\nsimplement semi-stable Y , de squelette S(Y) et de rétra tion τY : Y → S(Y). Dans es\n onditions :\n   \u0015 H = h|S(Y) ∈ H(S(Y), Γ(Y )) et h = H ◦ τY ;\n   \u0015 la fon tion F = log |f ||S(Y) est sous-harmonique sur S(Y) − Γ(Y ) (proposition 2.2.24,\n     (iii)) et log |f ||Y ≤ F ◦ τY (proposition 2.2.24, (ii)) ;\nla on lusion dé oule alors de l'inégalité F ≤ H sur S(Y).                                  2\nRemarque 3.1.7. \u0016 Nous verrons plus loin (proposition 3.4.6) que, pour toute S - ourbe\nsimplement semi-stable X et toute fon tion sous-harmonique u sur Xη − Γ(Xη ), la restri tion\nU de u à S(X ) − Γ(Xη ) est sous-harmonique (= onvexe) : ddc U ≥ 0, l'opérateur ddc étant\nentendu au sens des ourants, omme expliqué à en 1.2.5.\n\n     Les propriétés suivantes des fon tions sous-harmoniques sont évidentes.\nProposition 3.1.8. \u0016 Étant donné un ouvert Ω de X ,\n   (i ) l'ensemble des fon tions sous-harmoniques sur Ω est un \u001dne réel stable par max : pour\ntoutes fon tions u, v sous-harmoniques sur Ω et tous nombres réels positifs µ, ν , les fon tions\nµu + νv et max(u, v) sont sous-harmoniques sur Ω ;\n   (ii ) l'enveloppe inférieure d'une famille dé roissante de fon tions sous-harmoniques sur Ω\nest, sur ha une des omposantes onnexes de Ω, sous-harmonique ou identiquement égale à\n−∞ ;\n   (iii ) la régularisation semi- ontinue supérieurement de l'enveloppe supérieure d'une famille\n roissante et lo alement bornée de fon tions sous-harmoniques sur Ω est sous-harmonique.\n\n     Le \u001dne des fon tions sous-harmoniques sur un ouvert Ω de X est noté SH(Ω).",

      "literal": "  Les fon tions sous-harmoniques satisfont au prin ipe du maximum suivant.\n\nProposition 3.1.11. \u0016 Étant donné un ouvert Ω de X , une fon tion sous-harmonique u\nsur Ω admettant un maximum lo al en un point x de Ω est lo alement onstante au voisinage\nde x.\n\nDémonstration. Soit u une fon tion sous-harmonique admettant un maximum au point x\nde Ω et soit U un voisinage ouvert onnexe de x dans Ω tel que u|U ≤ u(x) ; puisque u|U n'est\npas identiquement égale à −∞, u(x) > −∞.\n   Soit Y un voisinage stri tement k-a\u001enoïde onnexe Y de x dans U . La restri tion de u à\nΓ(Y ) est onstante, égale à u(x) : s'il existait, en e\u001bet, un point y ∈ Γ(Y ) tel que u(y) < u(x),\nle lemme 2.3.9 fournirait une fon tion h ∈ H(Y ) telle que\n   \u0015 h(y) = u(y) ;\n   \u0015 h|Γ(Y )−{y} = u(x) ;\n\f64\n\n\n\npuisque Y est onnexe et h non onstante, le prin ipe du maximum pour les fon tions harmo-\nniques impliquerait alors h|Y −Γ(Y ) < u(x) et don , par sous-harmoni ité, l'inégalité absurde\nu(x) ≤ h(x) < u(x).\n   La on lusion est maintenant immédiate : l'ensemble des points de Shilov des voisinages\nstri tement k-a\u001enoïdes onnexes de x dans U étant une partie dense de U , la fon tion semi-\n ontinue supérieurement u|U est onstante, égale à u(x).                                     2\n\nCorollaire 3.1.12. \u0016 Soient Ω ⊂ X un ouvert et u : Ω → R ∪ {−∞} une fon tion semi-\n ontinue supérieurement qui n'est identiquement égale à −∞ sur au une omposante onnexe\nde Ω. Les propriétés suivantes sont équivalentes :\n   (i ) u est sous-harmonique ;\n   (ii ) pour tout ouvert Ω′ ⊂ Ω et pour toute fon tion harmonique h sur Ω′ , la fon tion u|Ω′ −h\nsatisfait au prin ipe du maximum (proposition 3.1.11) sur Ω′ .\nDémonstration. L'impli ation (i) ⇒ (ii) est l'appli ation dire te de la proposition pré é-\ndente puisque toute fon tion harmonique est sous-harmonique.\n   L'impli ation ré iproque est immédiate : étant donnés un domaine stri tement k-a\u001enoïde\nV ⊂ Ω et une fon tion h ∈ H(V ) telle que u|∂V ≤ h|∂V , l'appli ation du prin ipe du maximum\nà la fon tion u − h sur v − ∂V implique que elle- i atteint son maximum en un point de ∂V ;\nelle est don négative. La fon tion u est bien sous-harmonique sur Ω.                            2\n   La reformulation pré édente de la dé\u001cnition 3.1.5 met en éviden e le ara tère lo al de la\nsous-harmoni ité.\n\nCorollaire 3.1.13. \u0016 Le préfais eau SH sur |X| est un fais eau.\n\nDémonstration. La semi- ontinuité supérieure et le fait de ne pas être identiquement −∞\nsur une omposante onnexe sont des propriétés de nature lo ale. La propriété (ii) du orollaire\npré édent est également de nature lo ale.                                                  2\n\nProposition 3.1.14. \u0016 Soit X une ourbe stri tement k -analytique lisse.       "literal": "     In view of Theorem 7.1, it is natural to introduce as in [BJ18a]:\nDefinition 7.2. Assume that K is non-Archimedean, and let L be a semiample Q-line\nbundle. We say that a (possibly singular) metric φ on L is plurisubharmonic ( psh for short)\nif φ can be written as the pointwise limit of a decreasing net φi ∈ FS(L) of Fubini–Study\nmetrics on L. We denote by PSH(L) the set of psh metrics on L.\n   Given a subgroup Γ ⊂ R, nontrivial if K is trivially valued, FSΓ (L) is dense in FS(L)\nwith respect to uniform convergence, by Proposition 5.4 (v); it would thus be equivalent to\nrequire φi ∈ FSΓ (L) in the above definition.\n  As a direct consequence of Corollary 5.7, we have:\nLemma 7.3. Let A be the Stein factorization of L. Then\n                               PSH(A) ≃ PSH(L) ≃ PSH(Lred ).\n   The following general result, borrowed from [BJ18a, Proposition 5.6], ensures that PSH(L)\nis closed under decreasing limits.\nLemma 7.4. Let F ⊂ C0 (L) be a family of continuous metrics on a Q-line bundle L, closed\nunder addition of a constant, and define Fe as the set of possibly singular metrics on L that\ncan be written as decreasing limits of nets in F. Then Fe is closed under decreasing limits.\n                                           e For each i ∈ I choose a decreasing net (φi,j )j∈J\nProof. Let (φi )i∈I be a decreasing net in F.                                                  i\nin F converging to φi . Let A be the set of triples α = (i, j, m) with i ∈ I, j ∈ Ji and\nm ∈ Z>0 . For each α = (i, j, m) in A, set\n                                     ψα := φi,j + m−1 ∈ F,\nand define a partial order on A by\n                                     α ≥ α′ ⇐⇒ ψα ≤ ψα′ .\nWe claim that A is directed, i.e. any two elements a1 = (i1 , j1 , m1 ), a2 = (i2 , j2 , m2 ) in\nA can be dominated by a third. To see this, first pick i ≥ i1 , i2 , so that φi ≤ φi1 and\nφi ≤ φi2 , and set m := max{m1 , m2 } + 1. A simple variant of Dini’s lemma shows that\nφi,j + m−1 ≤ φik ,jk + m−1\n                         k = ψak on X\n                                       an for k = 1, 2 and all j ∈ J large enough, which\n                                                                      i\nproves the claim. By construction, (ψα )α∈A is a decreasing net in F such that ψα → φ\n                                  e\npointwise on X an , and hence φ ∈ F.                                                          \u0003\nCorollary 7.5. The space PSH(L) is stable under finite max and decreasing limits.\nCorollary 7.6. Pick a subgroup Γ ⊂ R, nontrivial if K is trivially valued. For any semi-\nample Q-line bundle L, the set C0 ∩ PSH(L) of continuous psh metrics on L is the closure\nof FSΓ (L) with respect to uniform convergence.\nProof. Let φ be a continuous psh metric, and pick a decreasing net (φi ) in FSΓ (L) converging\npointwise to φ. By Dini’s lemma, φi → φ uniformly on X an , and φ is thus in the closure\nof FSΓ (L). Conversely, let (φi ) be a sequence in FSΓ (L) converging unifornly to some\n(continuous) metric φ. We can inductively construct a sequence of constants ci → 0 such\nthat φi + ci is decreasing, and it follows that φ = limi (φi + ci ) is psh.                  \u0003\n\f                NORMS, DETERMINANT OF COHOMOLOGY AND FEKETE POINTS                           49\n\n7.2. Nef metrics. Assume that K is non-Archimedean. A model metric φ on a Q-line\nbundle L is nef if φ = φL for some Q-model L of L which is nef, i.e. L · C ≥ 0 for each\ne\nK-projective  curve C ⊂ Xs . By [GM19, Proposition 3.5], any Q-model L′ of L such that\nφ = φL′ is then also nef.\nDefinition 7.7. A nef metric on a Q-line bundle L is a continuous metric φ that can be\nwritten as a uniform limit of nef model metrics.\n   This is usually known as a semipositive metric in the sense of S.W. Zhang, cf. [GM19] for\na thorough discussion. When K is trivially valued, the trivial metric is the only nef metric.\nTheorem 7.8. Assume that K is nontrivially valued, and let L be an ample Q-line bundle.\nThen a continuous metric on L is nef iff it is psh (in the sense of Definition 7.2).\n   A proof of this result is also sketched, in a slightly different language, in [CMor18, Remark\n3.18]; we nevertheless provide a proof below, for completeness. By [GM19, Corollary 1.4],\nwe get:\nCorollary 7.9. Let L be an ample Q-line bundle, and pick a Q-model L of L. Then φL is\npsh iff L is nef.\nProof of Theorem 7.8. By Theorem 5.14, pure Fubini–Study metrics on L coincide with\nmodel metrics associated to Q-models L of L that are semiample, and hence nef. Since K\nis nontrivially valued, Corollary 7.6 implies that every continuous psh metric is a uniform\nlimit of pure Fubini–Study metrics, and hence is nef.\n   To prove the converse, let L be a nef Q-model of L, determined on a model X of X.\nUsing again Theorem 5.14, it will be enough to show that φL is a uniform limit of model\nmetrics associated to semiample Q-models. Since L is ample, it admits an ample Q-model\nA on some projective model X ′ dominating X (cf. [GM19, Lemma 4.12]). The pull-back\nL′ of L to X ′ is nef. For each ε ∈ (0, 1) ∩ Q, Lε := (1 − ε)L′ + εH is thus ample on Xs ,\nand hence on X , by [EGA, IV.9.6.4]. In particular, Lε is a semiample model of L. Since\nφL = φL′ (Lemma 5.10), φLε − φL = ε(φH − φL ) converges to 0 uniformly as ε > 0, and we\nare done.                                                                                 \u0003\nRemark 7.10. We expect Theorem 7.8 and Corollary 7.9 to hold when L is merely semi-\nample. For this, it would be enough to show that any nef model metric on L descends to the\nStein factorization A as in Lemma 5.5; this would for instance follow from a relative version\nof the local Hodge index theorem proved in [YZ16, Theorem 2.1].\n7.3. Psh-regularizable metrics. The curvature form of a smooth metric φ on a line bun-\ndle L is a closed (1, 1)-form, which we denote by ddc φ (see [CLD12, §6.4.1] for the non-\nArchimedean case). For each trivializing section τ of L over an open U ⊂ X, the function\nlog |τ |φ is smooth, and ddc φ = −ddc log |τ |φ on U an . Again, this makes sense for Q-line\nbundles as well.\nDefinition 7.11. A smooth metric φ on a Q-line bundle L is semipositive if its curvature\nform ddc φ is semipositive (see [CLD12, §5.4] for the non-Archimedean case).",

Mixed Monge–Ampère measures. (i) is straightforward. (ii) is classical in the Archimedean case, and proved in [CLD12,\nProposition 6.4.3] in the non-Archimedean case. It is further symmetric\nand multiadditive as a function of the φi .\n\f                    NORMS, DETERMINANT OF COHOMOLOGY AND FEKETE POINTS                                          57\n\n   Theorem 8.4 follows from the classical Bedford–Taylor theory [BT76] in the Archimedean\ncase, and from its analogue in the non-Archimedean case [CLD12, Corollaire 5.6.5]. In the\npresent global setting, one can however provide a simple direct argument, based on the\nfollowing global version of the classical Chern–Levine–Nirenberg inequality that will be put\nto use again a bit later.\nLemma 8.6. Let L0 , . . . , Ln be line bundles on X, and suppose given a pair of smooth,\nsemipositive metrics φi , φ′i on each Li .       "literal": "    In the theory of Chambert-Loir and Ducros [CD12, §5.4], we can associate to every\ncontinuous real function u on X an associated current [u]. The function h is called\nplurisubharmonic (or just psh) in the sense of Chambert-Loir and Ducros if the associated\ncurrent d′ d′′ [h] is positive. See [CD12, §5.5] for details. Then we can show the following\nmaximum principle.\nTheorem 1.7. Let X be a good, separated, strictly k-analytic space, and let u be a piecewise\nsmooth function on X which is psh in the sense of Chambert-Loir and Ducros. If u has a\nlocal maximum on x ∈ X \\ ∂ X , then u is locally constant at x.\n   Since any semipositive R-PL function is psh in the sense of Chambert-Loir and Ducros\n[GJR21, Theorem 7.14], the maximum principle holds for semipositive R-PL functions.\nWe prove Theorem 1.7 by proving a tropical analogue in Theorem 7.4: namely, if C ⊂\nRn is a tropical variety of pure dimension d and if f : C → R is weakly tropically psh,\nin the sense that the Lagerberg current d′ d′′ [ f ] is positive, then f satisfies a similar\nmaximum principle. The proof uses the Bieri–Groves theorem for affinoid varieties given\nin Theorem 1.6. Then Theorem 1.7 is shown in Theorem 7.7.\nRemark 1.8. We do not know whether an R-PL function that is psh in the sense of\nChambert-Loir and Ducros is necessarily semipositive. This is strongly related to the\nquestion of whether psh functions are stable under pullback, which is likewise unknown.",

GUBLER AND J. RABINOFF\n\nwhere the second inequality holds because κ(η) ⊂ H   f(z). Since z = p1 (v) = (p1 ◦ ψ)(s),\nwe have dk (s) ≥ dk (z) ≥ d. Since dim(S) = sups′ ∈S dk (s′ ) ≤ d, it follows that dim(S) =\ndk (s) = d.\n   Composing p1 with ψ: S → V ⊂ X ×k T0 gives a map p1 ◦ ψ: S → X . We claim that\nSz := (p1 ◦ ψ)−1(z) has dimension zero. If not, then dim(Sz ) ≥ 1, so there exists s′ ∈ Sz\nwith dH (z) (s′ ) ≥ 1. But then\n\n                       dk (s′ ) = dH (z) (s′ ) + dk (z) ≥ 1 + dk (z) ≥ 1 + d\n\n(the first equality is additivity in towers), which contradicts sups′ ∈S dk (s′ ) = dim(S) = d.\n   We claim now that p1 ◦ ψ is finite at s. Since s is isolated in its fiber, by [Ber93,\nProposition 3.1.4(c)] it is enough to show that p1 ◦ ψ is boundaryless at s. Since ϕ : S →\nT0 is boundaryless (it is finite) and since ψ: S → V factors ϕ, it follows from [Ber90,\nProposition 3.1.3(ii)] that ψ is boundaryless. We showed before that p1 is boundaryless\nat v, and the composition of two boundaryless morphisms is again boundaryless, so p1 ◦ψ\nis boundaryless, hence finite, at s.\n   Again by [Ber93, Proposition 3.1.4(b)], there exist affinoid neighborhoods U ′ of s and\nU of z such that p1 ◦ ψ induces a finite morphism U ′ → U. The image p1 ◦ ψ(U ′ ) is\ntherefore a Zariski-closed d-dimensional subspace Y of U containing z. Replacing Y be\nan irreducible component containing z (see [Duc18, §1.5]), we get the theorem. Here\nwe use that the dimension of a Zariski-closed subspace containing z is bounded below\nby d, as noted in Remark 4.2.                                                                \u0003\n\n   Let X be an analytic space and let x ∈ X . The infimum of the integers dimk {x}VZar\nfor V running through the set of analytic neighborhoods of x in X is called the central\ndimension of x in X and is denoted centdim(X , x) [Duc18, Definition 3.2.2]. For any\nanalytic neighborhood V of x in X one has dimk {x}VZar ≥ dk (x), as noted in Remark 4.2;\nit follows that centdim(X , x) ≥ dk (x).\n   A point has central dimension zero if and only if it is a rig-point. The following Corol-\nlary thus generalizes the fact that the rig-points are dense in an analytic space.\n\nCorollary 4.4. Let X be a good, strictly k-analytic space of pure dimension n. Then for any\nnonnegative integer d ≤ n, the set\n                         \b\n                           x ∈ X : centdim(X , x) = dk (x) = d\n\nis dense in X .\n\nProof. Let x ∈ X , let V be a strictly affinoid neighborhood of x, and let W be the interior\nof V in X ; this is an open neighborhood of x in X . Choose an admissible formal model V\nof V . The special fiber Vs has dimension n. Let E ⊂ Vs be an irreducible d-dimensional\nclosed subset and let η be its generic point. We apply Theorem 4.1 to V and W to get a\nneighborhood U of z in W , a point z ∈ W with reduction η, and a d-dimensional Zariski-\nclosed subspace Y ⊂ U with z ∈ Y . It follows that centdim(X , z) ≤ d, and equality holds\nby Remark 4.2.                                                                            \u0003",

      "literal": "                             5. SEMIPOSITIVE R-PL FUNCTIONS\n   Let k be a non-trivially valued non-Archimedean field. We consider a good, strictly\nk-analytic space X . We refer to 2.5 for the definition of PL functions. Recall from the in-\ntroduction that an R-PL function u: X → R is called semipositive at x ∈ X if the associated\nresidue line bundle Lu (x) ∈ Pic(red(X , x))R is nef, where we refer to [GJR21, 6.16–6.19]\nfor residue line bundles. Equivalently, there is a strictly affinoid neighbourhood U of x\nsuch that u = hL for a formal k◦ -model (U, L) of (U, OU ) with L nef [GJR21, Proposition\n7.1].\n   We will show first that for an R-PL function on a smooth curve, semipositivity is the\nsame as subharmonicity in the sense of Thuillier’s thesis [Thu05, Définition 3.1.5]. Re-\ncall that Thuillier defines a subharmonic function on a smooth k-analytic curve X as a\nsemicontinuous function u: X → R ∪ {−∞} which is not identically −∞ on any con-\nnected component of X and which has the property that for every harmonic function h\non a strictly affinoid domain U of X with u|∂ U ≤ h|∂ U , we have u|U ≤ h.\n\nProposition 5.1. Let X be a smooth, strictly k-analytic curve and let u: X → R be an R-PL\nfunction. Then u is subharmonic at x ∈ X in the sense of Thuillier if and only if Lu (x) is nef.\n\nProof. First we treat the case when k is algebraically closed. As explained in 2.5, there\nexists a compact strictly analytic neighborhood U of x in X , a formal k◦ -model U of U,\nand a formal model L of OU such that u|U = hL . Since k is algebraically closed, we may\nassume that the special fiber Us is reduced, which implies that U is covered by formal\naffines of the form Spf(A ◦ ) for a strictly k-affinoid algebra A [GJR21, §2.4]. It follows\nfrom [Ber90, Proposition 2.4.4] that for every irreducible component Y of Us , there is a\nunique point ξY ∈ U reducing to the generic point of Y . In the formalism of real valued\nforms and currents on Berkovich spaces [CD12], it is well known that d′ d′′ [u]|U is the\ndiscrete measure on U supported on the points ξY of U for Y an irreducible component\nof Us , with multiplicity at ξY equal to degL (Y ). See [CD12, §6.9] for a more general\nstatement. Wanner showed that subharmonicity of u on U is equivalent to the positivity\nof the current d′ d′′ [u]: see [Wan19, Theorems 1 and 2] and use the fact that X is locally\nalgebraic [vdP80]. Thus Ls is nef if and only if u is subharmonic on U. Suppose that u is\nsubharmonic at x. Shrinking U, we may assume that u is subharmonic on U. This implies\nthat Ls is nef, so it follows from [GJR21, Proposition 7.1] that Lu (x) is nef. Conversely, if\nLu (x) is nef, then by [GJR21, Proposition 7.1], we may shrink U so that Ls is nef, which\nas above implies that u is subharmonic on U.\n   It remains to show that subharmonicity of u at x and numerical effectivity of Lu (x)\ncan be checked after extending scalars to the completion of an algebraic closure K =\n(k)∧ . By [GJR21, Proposition 7.6(2)], one can check that Lh (x) is nef after base change\nto K. The same is true for subharmonicity: this follows directly from Thuillier’s definition\nand the fact that the base change of a harmonic function remains harmonic [Thu05,\nProposition 2.3.18].                                                                       \u0003\n\n  Our next goal is a characterisation of semipositive R-PL functions by restriction to\nsubcurves as in the complex case. We need the following characterization of boundary\npoints:\n\f16                                W. GUBLER AND J. RABINOFF\n\n5.2. Let X be an admissible formal k◦ -scheme and let x ∈ Xη . Then x ∈ Int(X ) = X \\ ∂ X\nif and only if the closure of redX (x) is a proper ek-scheme. This follows from [CD12,\nLemme 6.5.1] in the separated case and from [Vil21, Corollary A.4] in general.\nTheorem 5.3. Let X be a good, strictly k-analytic space, and let h: X → R be an R-PL\nfunction. For x ∈ X , the following conditions are equivalent.\n    (1) The function h is semipositive at x.\n    (2) There is a neighborhood W of x such for every strictly analytic neighborhood U ⊂ W\n        of x and every Zariski-closed curve C in U, the restriction h|C is semipositive.\nProof. Since semipositivity is an open property that is stable under pullback [GJR21,\nProposition 7.6], it is clear that (1) yields (2).\n   Assume that h satisfies (2). By shrinking W , we may assume that W is a strictly affinoid\nneighborhood of x and that there is a formal model W of U and a model L of OW on W\nwith coefficients in R such that h = hL on W . Let Y ⊂ Ws be a curve which is proper over\nthe residue field ek. To prove semipositivity at x, it is enough to show that degL (Y ) ≥ 0.\n   Choose any point y ∈ W reducing to the generic point η of Y . By Theorem 4.1 as\napplied to X = W and X = W, there exist z ∈ W with redW (z) = η, a neighborhood U\nof z in W , and an irreducible 1-dimensional Zariski-closed subspace C of U containing\nz. By shrinking U, we may assume that U is strictly affinoid. By results of Bosch and\nLütkebohmert [Bos14, Theorem 8.4.3] [BL93b, Corollary 5.4(b)], there is a formal model\nU of U and a morphism π: U → W extending the inclusion U ,→ W on generic fibers. Let\nC be the formal closure of C in U. This is an admissible formal model of C whose special\nfiber Cs is a closed 1-dimensional subscheme of Us containing η′ = redU (z). Functoriality\nof reductions shows that π(η′ ) = η, so the closure Y ′ of η′ is a curve mapping to Y .\nSince the closure of η in Ws is the proper curve Y , we conclude from 5.2 that z ∈ W \\\n∂ W . The boundary ∂ U is the union of ∂ W ∩ U with the topological boundary of U in\nW [Ber90, Proposition 3.1.3], so since U is a neighborhood of z in W , we have z ∈ U \\∂ U.\nApplying 5.2 to U and its formal model U shows that Y ′ is proper as well. Therefore,\nthe map Y ′ → Y is finite and surjective. Note that Y ′ is an irreducible component of Cs .\nApplying (2) to U and C, we have that h|C is semipositive, and so we deduce from [GJR21,\nLemma 7.4] as applied to (π∗L)|C that degπ∗ L (Y ′ ) ≥ 0. The projection formula then shows\ndegL (Y ) ≥ 0, as required.                                                                \u0003\n   To apply results from Thuillier’s thesis [Thu05], we would like to use only smooth an-\nalytic curves. To give a variant of the above result suitable for this purpose, we introduce\nthe following notations:\n   For a real function h on X and an analytic field extension F of k, we denote by h F\nthe induced function on the base change X F = X ⊗     b k F of X . We call an R-PL function\nh pluriharmonic if ±h are both semipositive. A strictly k-analytic space X is called rig-\nsmooth if the sheaf of Kähler differentials, considered on the G-topology, is locally free of\nrank equal to the local dimension. The space X is smooth if and only if X is rig-smooth and\nboundaryless. See [Duc18] for details (Ducros calls rig-smooth spaces quasi-smooth).\nCorollary 5.4. Let X be a good, strictly k-analytic space and let h: X → R be an R-PL\nfunction. Then the following conditions are equivalent:\n\f     ON SEMIPOSITIVE PIECEWISE LINEAR FUNCTIONS IN NON-ARCHIMEDEAN ANALYTIC GEOMETRY        17\n\n   (1) The function h is semipositive (resp. pluriharmonic).\n   (2) There is an algebraically closed analytic field extension F of k such that for every\n       rig-smooth analytic curve C over F and every morphism ϕ : C → X F , the function\n       h F ◦ ϕ is semipositive (resp. harmonic) on C.\n   (3) There is an algebraically closed analytic field extension F of k such that for every\n       smooth analytic curve C over F and every morphism ϕ : C → X F , the function h F ◦ϕ\n       is semipositive (resp. harmonic) on C.\n\n   If (2) or (3) is true for an algebraically closed analytic field extension F of k, then\nit is true for all analytic field extensions. This follows from Corollary 5.4 and Propo-\nsition [GJR21, Proposition 7.6(2’)] which allows to check semipositivity over any field\nextension.\n\nProof. It is enough to prove the claim for semipositive functions, as h is pluriharmonic if\nand only if h and −h are semipositive. Since semipositivity is stable under pullback and\nextension of scalars [GJR21, Proposition 7.6], clearly (1) yields (2). Since a smooth an-\nalytic curve is just a boundaryless rig-smooth curve, it is clear that (2) yields (3). In fact,\nthese two conditions are equivalent as an R-PL function is automatically semipositive in\nevery boundary point y of an analytic curve C. Indeed, it follows from 5.2 that red(C, y)\nis non-proper and hence there is no condition for semipositivity at y.\n   It remains to show that that (2) yields (1). Assume that (2) holds. By base change\nusing [GJR21, Proposition 7.6(2’)], we may assume that k = F . Let x ∈ X and let U be\na strictly analytic neighborhood of x. For any Zariski-closed curve Y in U, we will show\nthat h|Y is semipositive. Replacing Y by Yred and using [GJR21, Proposition 7.6(3’)], we\nmay assume that Y is reduced. Let ϕ : C → Y be the normalization of Y (see [Ber90]\nbefore Proposition 3.1.8). Then C is a rig-smooth curve because k is perfect (it is alge-\nbraically closed). By (2), the function h ◦ ϕ is semipositive on C. Since the normalization\nmorphism is finite and surjective, it follows from [GJR21, Proposition 7.6(1’)] that h|Y is\nsemipositive. By Theorem 5.3, we conclude that h is semipositive at x. Since x was an\narbitrary point of X , we get (1).                                                           \u0003\nCorollary 5.5. Let X be a good, strictly k-analytic space and let Λ be either Z or Q. Let\nh1 , h2 : X → R be semipositive Λ-PL functions. Then max(h1 , h2 ) is a semipositive Λ-PL\nfunction.",

      "literal": "Théorème 3.2. Soit X un espace k-analytique compact et Γ-strict de dimension d,\net soit f = (f1 , . . . , fn ) une famille de fonctions analytiques inversibles sur X ;\nnotons |f | l’application (|f1 |, . . . , |fn |) de X vers (R∗+ )n . Posons c = (Q, (|k ∗ | · Γ)Q ).\n1) L’image |f |(X) est un c-polytope de (R∗+ )n , qui est de dimension inférieure ou\n   égale à d.\n2) L’image |f |(∂X) est contenue dans un c-polytope de (R∗+ )n qui est de dimension\n   inférieure ou égale à d − 1 ; lorsque X est aﬃnoı̈de |f |(∂X) est elle-même un c-\n   polytope de (R∗+ )n de dimension inférieure ou égale à d − 1.\n    Démonstration. La seconde assertion se déduira de la première.\n(3.2.1) Preuve de 1) : réduction à une situation algébrique. On peut supposer\nque X est aﬃnoı̈de et irréductible, et l’on procède alors par récurrence sur d. Si d = 0\nil n’y a rien à démontrer. Supposons que d > 0 et que 1) est vraie en dimension < d.\n     Le théorème à établir est purement topologique. On peut donc supposer k parfait\n(en étendant les scalaires au complété de la clôture radicelle de k), et X réduit (en\nle remplaçant par Xred ). L’espace X est alors génériquement quasi-lisse. Soit Y son\nlieu de non-quasi-lissité. C’est un fermé de Zariski de X dont toutes les composantes\nirréductibles sont de dimension < d. Par hypothèse de récurrence, |f |(Y ) est un\nc-polytope de (R∗+ )n de dimension ⩽ d − 1. Son image réciproque |f |−1 (|f |(Y )) est\nun domaine analytique Γ-strict de X contenant Y . Comme Y \b→ X est sans bord, Y\nest contenu dans l’intérieur topologique de |f |−1 (|f |(Y )). Ce dernier apparaı̂t ainsi\ncomme un voisinage de Y dont l’image par |f | est un c-polytope de dimension\n⩽ d − 1.\n     Soit x ∈ X − Y . Comme X est quasi-lisse en x, le lemme 0.21 assure l’existence\nd’un voisinage aﬃnoı̈de V de x dans X, et d’une k-variété aﬃne X de dimension d,\ntelle que V s’identiﬁe à un domaine aﬃnoı̈de Γ-strict de X an (on peut imposer à X\nd’être lisse, mais nous ne nous en servirons pas).\n     Par compacité de X, on se ramène ﬁnalement au cas où X lui-même est est un\ndomaine aﬃnoı̈de Γ-strict de l’analytiﬁcation X an d’une k-variété algébrique X\naﬃne de dimension d.\n     Écrivons X ≃ Spec A où A est une k-algèbre de type ﬁni, et soit (a1 , . . . , ar )\nun système de générateurs de A. Il existe R ∈ Γ · |k ∗ | tel que X soit contenu\ndans le domaine aﬃnoı̈de W de X an déﬁni par les conditions |ai | ⩽ R. En vertu\ndu théorème de Gerritzen-Grauert (0.20.7), le domaine X est réunion ﬁnie de\ndomaines Γ-rationnels de W ; il suﬃt dès lors de traiter le cas où X est lui-même\nun domaine Γ-rationnel de W .\n     Il est alors déﬁni par une conjonction d’inégalités de la forme |gi | ⩽ λi |h| où les λi\nappartiennent à Γ et où (g1 , . . . , gr , h) engendre l’idéal des fonctions analytiques\nsur W . Cette dernière condition implique que h ne s’annule pas sur X (tout zéro\nde h sur X est un zéro commun à h et aux gi ), et l’on peut donc, par compacité,\n                                            1250007-40\n\f                            Espaces de Berkovich, polytopes, squelettes et théorie des modèles\n\n\nrajouter une condition de la forme |h| ⩾ r à la déﬁnition de X, où r est un élément\nconvenable de (Γ · |k ∗ |)Q . Il est dès lors immédiat que V ne change pas si l’on\nperturbe un peu les gi et h ; on peut par conséquent supposer que toutes ces\nfonctions appartiennent à A.\n    L’anneau des fonctions de la forme a/h\u0005 , où a ∈ A, et \u0010 ⩾ 0, est dense dans\nl’anneau des fonctions de X. Cela permet de supposer, quitte à remplacer X\npar D(h), à remplacer gi par gi /h et à approcher convenablement chacune des fi ,\nque fi ∈ A pour tout i et que h = 1.\n(3.2.2) Nature c-polytopale de |f |(X) dans le cas algébrique. Soit (F, |.|) une\nextension valuée de k (on ne suppose pas que le groupe ordonné |F ∗ | est de\nrang 1). On peut donner un sens naturel à la notation X(F ), coı̈ncidant avec sa\nsigniﬁcation usuelle lorsque F est un corps ultramétrique complet : c’est le sous-\nensemble de X (F ) formé des points P tels que |gi (P )| ⩽ λi pour tout i.\n    Le théorème 1.2 assure l’existence d’un sous-foncteur fermé et déﬁnissable D\nde GnΓ·|k∗ | , de dimension majorée par d (les notations et déﬁnitions sont celles de\n0.32 et sq.) tel que\n                             |f |(X(F )) = D(|F ∗ |) ⊂ |F ∗ |n\npour tout F ∈ Ck,Γ·|k∗ | .\n     Si F est une extension ultramétrique complète et algébriquement close de k\nmunie d’un |k ∗ |-isomorphisme |F ∗ | ≃ R∗+ , tout point P de X(F ) déﬁnit un point x\nde l’espace k-aﬃnoı̈de X, et |f |(x) = |f |(P ) ∈ (R∗+ )n .\n     Réciproquement si x ∈ X il existe une extension complète et algébriquement\nclose F de H (x) et un |H (x)∗ |-isomorphisme |F ∗ | ≃ R∗+ , et x est alors induit par\nun F -point canonique de X.\n     En conséquence, le compact |f |(X) est le sous-ensemble D(R∗+ ) de (R∗+ )n , ce\nqui achève de prouver 1).\n                                                                                  \u0005\n(3.2.3) Démonstration de l’assertion 2. Si X s’écrit comme une réunion ﬁnie Xi\noù les Xi sont aﬃnoı̈des et Γ-stricts, son bord est contenu dans la réunion des bords\ndes Xi . On peut donc supposer que X est aﬃnoı̈de, auquel cas le résultat découle\nde l’assertion 1) déjà établie et du lemme 3.1.\n(3.3) Remarque. Faute d’avoir réussi à exhiber une description suﬃsamment\nmaniable de ∂X en général, l’auteur ignore s’il peut arriver que |f |(∂X) ne soit\npas un polytope, lorsque X n’est pas aﬃnoı̈de. Il serait évidemment intéressant de\ndisposer d’un tel exemple — ou, au contraire, d’une preuve que |f |(∂X) est toujours\nun polytope.",

      "literal": "Lemme 5.5.2]).\n   By [CD12, Lemme 5.5.3], a smooth function h is psh if and only if every x ∈ X has a\n                                                                                r,an\ncompact strictly analytic neighbourhood V with a moment map ϕ : V → Gm               such that\nh|V = u◦ϕtrop for a continuous function u on ϕtrop (V ) which restricts to a convex function\non each face. This characterization shows that smooth psh functions admit pull-backs\nwith respect to morphisms.\nTheorem 7.14. Let X be a good separated strictly analytic space over K and let h: X → R\nbe a semipositive R-PL function. Then h restricts to a psh function on X \\ ∂ X .\nProof. By restriction to X \\ ∂ X , we may assume that X is boundaryless. The claim is local\nin X and so we may assume that h is a uniform limit of R-PL LFS functions hi on X by\nusing Proposition 7.10. It follows from 7.9 and from [CD12, Corollaire 6.3.2] that an\nR-PL locally Fubini–Study function is locally a uniform limit of smooth psh functions and\nhence hi is psh. For any positive form ω ∈ Asm,c (X ), we deduce from uniform convergence\nthat                                 Z                  Z\n                      〈ω, d′ d′′ [h]〉 =        h · d′ d′′ ω = lim       hi · d′ d′′ ω ≥ 0.\n                                                              i\n                                           X                        X\n  6\n    Currents in analysis work well only in the boundaryless case, as otherwise there are disturbing residues\nalong the boundary. Note that Chambert-Loir and Ducros also omit the boundary in their definition of\ncurrents [CD12, 4.2.2], as their currents act on smooth forms with compact support disjoint from the\nboundary.\n\f50                                W. GUBLER, P. JELL, AND J. RABINOFF\n\nWe conclude that h is psh.                                                                          \u0003\nCorollary 7.15. Let h be a harmonic function on a good separated strictly analytic space X\nover K. Then the restriction of the current d′ d′′ [h] to X \\ ∂ X is zero.\nProof. We may assume that X is boundaryless. It follows from Theorem 7.14 that h and\n−h are psh on X . By linearity in the irreducible components and by arguing locally, we\nmay assume that X is of pure dimension d. Let η ∈ Asm,c             d−1,d−1\n                                                                            (X ). Since d′ d′′ [h] is a\nsymmetric current on X , we have\n                           1                                             \u0001 1\n         〈η, d′ d′′ [h]〉 =    〈η, d′ d′′ [h]〉 + (−1)d−1 〈Jη, d′ d′′ [h]〉 = 〈ω, d′ d′′ [h]〉\n                           2                                                  2\nfor the symmetric form ω := η + (−1)d−1 Jη ∈ Asm,c       d−1,d−1\n                                                                 (X ). By Lemma 7.12, there are\n                             d−1,d−1\npositive forms ω1 , ω2 ∈ Asm,c (X ) such that ω = ω1 − ω2 . As h and −h are psh, we get\n                 2〈η, d′ d′′ [h]〉 = 〈ω1 , d′ d′′ [h]〉 − 〈ω2 , d′ d′′ [h]〉 = 0 − 0 = 0,\nproving the claim.                                                                                  \u0003\nRemark 7.16. In this section, we have focused on the concept of semipositive and har-\nmonic functions. In fact, all the results immediately transfer to Λ-PL metrics k k of a\nline bundle L over a good strictly analytic space X . Indeed, the choice of a local frame s\ngives rise to a Λ-PL function h := − log ksk, to which we can apply the above results. Then\nTheorem 7.14 yields that on a good separated strictly analytic space X every semipositive\nR-PL metric of L has a positive first Chern current c1 (L, k k) as defined in [CD12, 6.4.1].\nThe same holds for uniform limits of such metrics on L which includes all the metrics\nconsidered by Zhang [Zha95] over projective varieties.",
